<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For each fixed rotating subextremal Kerr background with mass <i>M</i> &gt; 0 and rotation $`0\lt |\mathfrak a|\lt M`$, we prove that the smooth vacuum data near its complete two-ended bridge whose full maximal globally hyperbolic development admits a future $`C^0\cap W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}`$ extension form a meagre set in the weighted smooth topology. The extension metric is continuous and nondegenerate, and its weak connection is locally square-integrable. The neighborhood requires smallness of only the tenth weighted seminorm, while the topology tests every finite order.

- **claim-generic-weak-inextendibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed $M>0$ and $0<\|\mathfrak a\|<M$, there is $\varepsilon=\varepsilon(M,\mathfrak a)>0$ such that the set of $d\in\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$ for which the full development $(M_d,g_d)$ admits a future extension in the sense of Definition <a href="#intro:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:extension">1</a> is meagre in $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$. Consequently, future $C^0\cap W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$-inextendible data contain a residual subset of $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$ in the weighted smooth topology <a href="#intro:seminorms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:seminorms">[intro:seminorms]</a>.
  - Explanation: Near each fixed rotating subextremal Kerr bridge, future extendibility with a continuous nondegenerate metric and locally square-integrable weak connection is meagre in the weighted smooth topology. The conclusion concerns the full maximal development.
- **claim-background-estimate-package** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bg-package)): For every $d\in\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$, the comparison geometry has the following properties.* 1. *The metrics $\gamma^{\pm1}$ and $\mathcal A^{\pm1}$ are bounded in angular $H^6$, and $\xi_i,a^{-1}\partial_vb$ are bounded in angular $H^5$. There are nonnegative measurable functions $g_i:\mathbb R\to[0,\infty)$ with $$\label{bg:cell-sum} \sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}\sup_{[n,n+1]}g_i<\infty$$ such that $$\label{bg:directional-low} \norm{\chi_i}_{H^4}+\norm{\ell_i+\kappa}_{H^4} +\norm{B_i}_{H^5} \le C\bigl(q+g_i(y_i)\bigr).$$ The corresponding first pure longitudinal derivatives $\mathfrak L_i\chi_i,L_i\ell_i$, and $\partial_u b$ in direction $0$, have directional summable bounds through angular $H^2$, after enlarging the $g_i$.* 2. *The first ordinary longitudinal jets satisfy $$\label{bg:low-jets} \begin{gathered} \norm b_{H^5}+\sum_i\norm{(\chi_i,\ell_i+\kappa)}_{H^4}\to0,\\ \sum_{k=0}^1\left(\norm{\partial_{y_k}b}_{H^2} +\sum_i\norm{\partial_{y_k}(\chi_i,\ell_i)}_{H^2}\right)\to0,\\ \sum_{k=0}^1\left(\norm{\partial_{y_k}\gamma}_{H^4} +\norm{\partial_{y_k}\log a+\kappa}_{H^4}\right)\to0 \end{gathered}$$ as $t\to\infty$ and $\|u\|,\|v\|\to\infty$. The displayed norms are bounded everywhere in the wedge.* 3. *For fixed $U\in\mathbb R$, $C>0$, and each fixed ordinary coordinate derivative order, the fields $$\label{bg:high-fields} \gamma^{\pm1},\quad\log\mathcal A,\quad b, \quad a^{-1}\partial_vb$$ have $e^{o(T)}$ bounds on $$\label{bg:truncated-wedge} u\le U,\qquad v\le CT,\qquad t\ge0.$$ The assertion holds for either optical ordering and on restricted coordinate-path segments. Fixed derivatives of $a$, $\sqrt a$, and $\partial_vb$ retain respectively the factors $a$, $\sqrt a$, and $a$, with subexponential coefficient bounds.
  - Explanation: The comparison wedge has bounded low angular geometry, summable directional envelopes, controlled longitudinal jets, and subexponential bounds at every fixed higher order. Higher-order constants may depend on the fixed datum.
- **claim-first-runout-and-corner-nullity** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-bg-runout)): Every seed whose future geodesic has finite maximal proper lifetime in $M_d$ crosses $\mathcal S$. Its first runout from $\mathcal P_d$ is either a branch, where one optical coordinate has a finite limit and the other tends to $+\infty$, or a corner, where both tend to $+\infty$. For each fixed datum, seeds whose first runout is a corner form a $\mu_d$-null set.
  - Explanation: Finite full future lifetime forces a crossing of the comparison cylinder. First comparison runout is a branch or a corner, and corner seeds are null for each fixed datum. Comparison runout need not coincide with the full endpoint.
- **claim-uniform-branch-observation-tube** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-bg-branch-tube)): Fix a branch seed, with optical ordering $u\to u_*\in\mathbb R$, $v\to\infty$. There is a relatively compact open neighborhood $\mathcal N$ of this seed, as small as desired, with the following properties.* 1. *All seeds in $\overline{\mathcal N}$ have an initial compact flow tube from $\Sigma$ to a fixed late $v$ section, followed by tracks contained in a common compact $u$ range and a sphere coordinate chart. The section hits in the earlier flow tube are transverse and single-valued. In canonical state variables $(u,\theta,\pi_u,P)$, both momenta are bounded and $$\label{bg:p} p=-(\pi_u+b^AP_A)=a\dot v$$ has positive upper and lower bounds. Each track has branch runout in this ordering.* 2. *For a sufficiently small fixed interval $J$ about zero and an unbounded sequence of $T$, there are product observation boxes $\mathcal Q_T$ of fixed side lengths in $(u,v-T,\theta)$ containing all hitting positions of these tracks at $v\in T+J$. The closure of a fixed enlargement stays in the common $u$ and angular-chart margins. The angular feet on $\mathcal S$ of the $L_0$ segments from every point of that enlarged closure lie, with a fixed interior margin, in one admissible small packet launch patch. The patch size is fixed by the low-order ray and collar construction, independently of later derivative and accuracy choices.* 3. *For fixed $u_2>u_*$, $c_2>0$, and sufficiently small $c_0>0$, the neighborhood, $J$, and box sizes can be chosen so that all entry-to-observation track portions and enlarged observation boxes fit with strict outer margins in $$\label{bg:experiment-domain} \begin{gathered} 0\le t\le L_T=(T+u_*)/2+c_0,\\ u\le u_2-c_0t/T, \qquad v\le T+c_2-c_0t/T. \end{gathered}$$ The slice $x$ intervals have a positive lower length, uniformly for large $T$. No margin from the entry surface $t=0$ is required.
  - Explanation: A branch seed has an arbitrarily small open seed neighborhood with uniform canonical state bounds, observation boxes, an admissible launch footprint, and strict finite experiment margins.
- **claim-interior-causal-homotopies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ex-homotopy)): Let $p,q\in M$ and let $H:[0,1]\times[0,1]\longrightarrow\widetilde M$ be continuous. Suppose that every $H(r,\cdot)$ is a locally Lipschitz future causal path from $p$ to $q$, with causal tangent almost everywhere, and that $H(0,[0,1])\subset M$. Then the entire image of $H$ lies in $M$.
  - Explanation: A continuous family of future causal paths with common interior endpoints remains inside the original spacetime when its initial member does. Compact intrinsic causal diamonds supply the closedness argument.
- **claim-lipschitz-first-exit-graph** (construction, [source](#stmt-ex-graph)): Suppose that a future timelike $C^1$ curve exits $M$ as in Definition <a href="#intro:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:extension">1</a>. There is a precompact ambient coordinate chart with coordinates $(s,z)\in\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^3$, a constant $c>0$, a bottom height $-d_0<0$, a spatial ball $D$, and a Lipschitz function $f:D\to\mathbb R$, with Lipschitz constant $L<\infty$, having the following properties:* 1. *$s$ is temporal, and every vector $(1,\eta)$ with $\|\eta\|<c$ is uniformly future timelike;* 2. *$(-d_0,z)\in I_M^+(\Sigma)$ for all $z\in D$;* 3. *the vertical segment $\{(s,z):-d_0\le s<f(z)\}$ lies in $M$, while $(f(z),z)\in\partial M$;* 4. *$f(0)=0$ and, for some $0<\delta<d_0/4$, $-\delta<f(z)\le\delta$. The rectangle containing these segments has an upper face above $2\delta$.* *The metric, its inverse, the positive spatial block $(g_{ij})$, and the timelike normalization on any closed narrower cone have uniform bounds and uniform nondegeneracy on this rectangle.
  - Explanation: An actual future timelike exit admits a local Lipschitz first-exit graph, a common interior bottom, a strict timelike cone, and uniform algebraic metric bounds. Intrinsic global hyperbolicity is sufficient.
- **claim-small-normalized-connection-boxes** (construction, [source](#stmt-ex-boxes)): Fix $K>1$ and a ball $D'\Subset D$ from [ex:graph](#ex:graph). There are $l_n\downarrow0$ and $z_n\in D'$ such that the boxes $$Q_l(z)=\{(s,y):\|y-z\|<Kl,\ \|s-f(z)\|<Kl\}$$ lie in one fixed precompact subchart and satisfy $$\label{ex:small-box} l_n^{-3}\int_{Q_{l_n}(z_n)} \|\widetilde\Gamma\|\,\mathrm ds\,\mathrm dy \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Averaging over centers near the exit graph produces shrinking boxes whose normalized connection mass tends to zero. Absolute continuity of the connection integral and the graph's null volume are essential.
- **claim-positive-family-from-weak-exit** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-exit-family)): Under the hypotheses of [ex:graph](#ex:graph), there is a measurable set $S\subset T\Sigma$ of positive smooth section measure such that every future unit geodesic with seed in $S$ has finite maximal future proper lifetime in the full $M$ and an ambient endpoint on $\partial M$. The endpoints lie in one compact subset of an ambient chart. Moreover, there are finitely many precompact ambient charts $\psi_j:U_j\to V_j\subset\mathbb R^4$ and constants $C<\infty$, $m>0$ with the following properties:* 1. *on each entire $V_j$, the metric and inverse metric have component norms at most $C$, and $\\|\widetilde\Gamma_j\\|_{L^2(V_j)}\le C$;* 2. *for every seed in $S$ and every proper time from its intersection with $\Sigma$ to its full future endpoint, excluding only the endpoint itself, at least one $j$ satisfies $$\label{ex:chart-witness} \operatorname{dist}\bigl(\psi_j(\gamma(\tau)), \mathbb R^4\setminus V_j\bigr)\ge m, \qquad \|(\psi_j)_*\dot\gamma(\tau)\|\le C.$$* *Here the section measure is the pullback of canonical volume on $T^*\Sigma$ under $w\mapsto h(w,\cdot)$, where $h$ is the induced metric. In particular it has the same null sets as every smooth positive measure on $T\Sigma$.
  - Explanation: Every weak future exit supplies a positive section-measure family of smooth interior timelike geodesics with finite full lifetime and ambient boundary endpoints. Uniform chart margins, velocities, metric bounds, and connection norms hold along their entire future arcs.
- **claim-countable-cover-of-extensions** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-exit-cover)): Every datum in $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$ admitting a future $C^0\cap W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ extension belongs to some $E(O,B)$. Consequently the extendible set is precisely $$\bigcup_{O\in\mathcal B}\ \bigcup_{B=1}^{\infty}E(O,B).$$ This cover includes extensions whose actual exit occurs after first runout from the comparison development.
  - Explanation: All future weakly extendible data are exactly covered by the countably indexed existential extension tests. The cover also handles actual exits occurring after first comparison runout.
- **claim-exact-packet-preparation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-packet-preparation)): For every finite $m\ge10$, finite jet budget $N$, positive loss tolerance $\eta_{\rm out}$, and $0<\nu<1$, one can choose $\zeta>0$ and finite construction orders so that the following objects exist for arbitrarily large $T$ along the indicated sequence.* 1. *There are exact smooth complete vacuum data $d_T\in\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge with $p_m(d_T-d)\to0$. They agree with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and an expanding protected ball on the selected end. The permitted exterior correction tail is retained.* 2. *An exact exterior evolution of $d_T$ reaches the required part of $\mathcal S$, with overlapping collars and strict outflow margins. In the fixed background wave-map description its entry spacetime jets differ from $g+h^p$ by $O(A_T^2e^{\eta_{\rm out} T})$. Here $$\label{pk:packet} h^p=A_T\,\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\operatorname{Re} \left(e^{i\lambda v}\sum_{j=0}^{J_*}\lambda^{-j}p_j\right).$$ The leading coefficient is real, trace-free, and annihilates $L_0$. On the entire observation box $\mathcal Q_T$, $$\label{pk:screen-lower} \|(p_0)_{AB}\|_\gamma\ge e^{-o(T)}.$$ At every fixed required base order the ordinary mixed coordinate jets of the normalized amplitude components are $e^{o(T)}$, and the linearized Ricci and gauge errors have arbitrarily high inverse-frequency accuracy.* 3. *There is a metric $\bar g$ exactly of double-null form, with fields $\bar\gamma,\log\bar{\mathcal A},\bar b$ and their exact geometric derived fields. Through order $N$, its differences from the background fields, including $q^{-1}\partial_vb$, are $O(A_Te^{\eta_{\rm out} T})$. Its defects in the null structure and angular equations <a href="#dn:D2" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dn:D2">[dn:D2]</a>–<a href="#dn:D3" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="dn:D3">[dn:D3]</a> are $O(A_T^2e^{\eta_{\rm out} T})$. If the actual exterior induced data are written in double-null coordinates initialized by the background values on $\mathcal S$, their full entry jets differ from this template by the same quadratic bound.* *All entry discrepancies and all template changes and defects vanish for $v<(1-\nu)T$. Any additional finite initial or residual jet buffers may be included in $N$. Positive frequency powers at these base orders are independent of the later orders used to improve linear accuracy. At the finitely many high entry orders ultimately requested by [thm:dn-comparison](#thm:dn-comparison), the actual fields have $e^{o(T)}$ bounds.
  - Explanation: The preparation produces exact complete vacuum perturbations, controlled exterior entry jets, a packet polarized throughout the observation footprint, and an exact double-null template with quadratic defects. Protected agreement and the correction tail are retained.
- **claim-linear-optical-gauge-and-relative-shift** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge)): Let $Z=(z^0,z^1,z^\theta)$ be the variations of the coordinate functions, with $Z=0$ on $\mathcal S$, for the input $h^p$. In unnormalized components $h^p_{ij}=h^p(L_i,L_j)$ and $h^p_{iA}=h^p(L_i,\partial_A)$, they obey $$\label{pk:linear-optical} \begin{aligned} \partial_vz^0&=-\frac{h^p_{11}}{2a},& L_0z^1&=-\frac{h^p_{00}}{2a},\\ \partial_vz^\theta &=b\partial_vz^0+a\gamma^{-1}\mathrm d_\theta z^0 +\gamma^{-1}h^p_{1\cdot}. \end{aligned}$$ For every fixed number of angular derivatives and $r$ total longitudinal derivatives, $$\label{pk:coordinate-gain} \|\partial_{u,v}^{r}\partial_\theta^sZ\| \le A_T\lambda^{r-1}e^{o(T)}.$$ The fixed-new-label metric variation is $k=h^p-\mathcal L_Zg$. Its field variations $k_\gamma,k_H,k_b$, where $H=\log\mathcal A$, and the relative derivative $q^{-1}\partial_vk_b$ satisfy $$\label{pk:relative-linear} \\|(k_\gamma,k_H,k_b,q^{-1}\partial_vk_b)\\|_{C^s} \le A_T\lambda^{P_s}e^{o(T)}$$ at every fixed required order. The exponents $P_s$ depend only on that order, not on subsequent inverse-accuracy choices.
  - Explanation: Linear optical coordinate variations gain inverse frequency. The associated relative field changes, including the relative shift derivative, have controlled fixed-order frequency losses independent of later accuracy choices.
- **claim-finite-double-null-vacuum-experiment** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-dn-comparison)): For every fixed background and branch tube in [lem:bg-branch-tube](#lem:bg-branch-tube), every finite $m,s$, and every sufficiently small fixed $c>0$, the parameters in [prop:packet-preparation](#prop:packet-preparation) can be chosen so that its exact entry data have a smooth vacuum development on [dn:domain](#dn:domain) in the double-null gauge initialized there. The relative lapse and the sphere metric remain positive and uniformly nondegenerate. For $$\mathcal U=(\gamma,\log\mathcal A,b,\chi_0,\chi_1, \ell_0,\ell_1,\xi_0,\xi_1)$$ one has, in a fixed sphere atlas and ordinary coordinate derivatives, $$\label{dn:main-error} \max_{\|I\|\le s}\\|\partial^I(\mathcal U'-\bar{\mathcal U}) \\|_{L^\infty(\Omega_T)} \le e^{-(1+c)\alpha T}$$ for all sufficiently large $T$ in the selected experiment sequence. The same conclusion holds for $q^{-1}\partial_vb'-q^{-1}\partial_v\bar b$ at any preassigned finite order, after increasing the finite order budget. The individual ordinary jets of the primed relative fields through every order used in the experiment are $e^{o(T)}$, uniformly for this moving family. The exact solution and the template equal the background where $v<(1-\nu)T$. The exact solution agrees on an entry collar, by local geometric uniqueness, with the actual exterior solution of [prop:packet-preparation](#prop:packet-preparation).
  - Explanation: Actual prepared entry data evolve through the finite double-null domain with uniform nondegeneracy and an error smaller than the packet amplitude at every prescribed finite order. The template is a comparison metric rather than an exact vacuum solution.
- **claim-uniform-high-jets-on-partial-slabs** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-dn-high-uniform)): Assume [dn:bootstrap](#dn:bootstrap) on an existing initial partial slab $\Omega_T\cap\{0\le t\le t_*\}$ of [dn:domain](#dn:domain), with the same outer margins. For every fixed finite order $n$ there is a finite initial order $r(n)$ with the following property. Uniform $e^{o(T)}$ initial bounds through order $r(n)$ imply uniform $e^{o(T)}$ bounds through order $n$ for all ordinary jets of $$\gamma'^{\pm1},\quad\log\mathcal A',\quad b',\quad \chi_i',\quad\ell_i',\quad\xi_i',\quad q^{-1}\partial_vb'.$$ The constants depend only on the fixed orders, a prescribed exponential-rate tolerance, the bounded low coefficient set, the uniform envelope bounds described below, and finitely many initial norms. They do not depend on an uncontrolled high norm of the unknown solution or on the subslab endpoint.
  - Explanation: Under the low bootstrap, sufficiently high finite initial bounds propagate subexponential bounds for all requested ordinary relative-field jets. The constants avoid dependence on uncontrolled high norms and the partial-slab endpoint.
- **claim-quadratic-low-comparison-error** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-dn-low-error)): For every $\eta_2>0$, the input loss $\eta$ and the support fraction $\nu$ may be chosen sufficiently small, with the finite base orders fixed, so that on every existing bootstrap subslab $$\label{dn:low-conclusion} \sup_{0\le t\le L_T} \\| (\Delta\gamma,\Delta\log\mathcal A,\Delta b, \Delta\chi_i,\Delta\ell_i,\Delta\xi_i) \\|_{L^2(I_T(t)\times\mathbb S^2)} \le A_T^2e^{\eta_2T}.$$ The supremum is restricted to available slices. At the same times, $$\label{dn:low-auxiliary-conclusion} \sum_i\\|Y_i+Z_i+z_i\\|_{L^2(I_T(t))} +\\|(\nabla\Delta\gamma,\nabla\Delta b) \\|_{L^2(I_T(t)\times\mathbb S^2)} \le A_T^2e^{\eta_2T}.$$
  - Explanation: Small input loss and late support make the actual-minus-template slice error quadratic in packet amplitude with arbitrarily small exponential loss. The angular estimates retain the lapse factors needed to compensate weighted curvature norms.
- **claim-whole-bridge-experiment-attachment** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-experiment-attachment)): For sufficiently large finite $T$, the exact exterior and deep experiments, restricted inside their construction margins, lie in a smooth globally hyperbolic vacuum development of the entire datum $d_T$. This development embeds isometrically in its full MGHD $M_{d_T}$. The full initial bridge and the exterior correction tail are retained. The common earlier branch tubes and the compact observation region have interior margins in the attached region.
  - Explanation: The actual exterior and deep experiments join into a smooth globally hyperbolic development of the entire corrected bridge datum. Maximal-development uniqueness places the finite observation region in the full development.
- **claim-whole-patch-relative-curvature-signal** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-curvature-signal)): At every point of $\mathcal Q_T$ with $\|\cos(\lambda v)\|\ge1/2$, one has, for all large $T$, $$\label{at:signal} \|D(g_T)-D(g)\|>2A_T.$$ The norm is the ordinary angular component norm. No bound on $D(g)$, or on background parallel-frame curvature, is assumed.
  - Explanation: On the cosine-good part of the whole observation patch, the exact experiment's curvature block differs from the background by more than the stated packet threshold. The proof needs no bound on background parallel-frame curvature.
- **claim-matched-canonical-seeds-and-persistence** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-matched-seeds)): For $v\in T+J_0$, use the same numerical states <a href="#at:terminal-state" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="at:terminal-state">[at:terminal-state]</a> in $g_T$ on its future unit shell. Their backward geodesics give maps $\Psi_{T,v}:O_0\to O$ with $$\label{at:seed-closeness} \sup_{v\in T+J_0}\\|\Psi_{T,v}-\operatorname{incl}\\|_{C^1} \longrightarrow0.$$ Write $\delta_T$ for the left side of <a href="#at:seed-closeness" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="at:seed-closeness">[at:seed-closeness]</a>. Each map is injective and preserves the corresponding section measure: $\Psi_{T,v}^*\mu_{d_T}=\mu_d$ on $O_0$. In particular $\mathcal H_v(O_0)$ has canonical volume $\mu_d(O_0)$ for every such $v$.* *Put $$\label{at:frame} r_T=e^{-\kappa T},\qquad F=(\partial_u,r_T^{-1}\partial_v, \partial_{\theta^1},\partial_{\theta^2}).$$ For $g$ and $g_T$ on the larger observation box, the Gram matrices in $F$ and their inverses are uniformly bounded and nondegenerate. Their ordinary coordinate jets through order thirty are $e^{o(T)}$. The selected unit tangent is bounded in $F$, all state momenta lie in one bounded box, and $$\label{at:volume} c r_T\,\mathrm du\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm d^2\theta \le \mathrm d\operatorname{vol}_g,\ \mathrm d\operatorname{vol}_{g_T} \le C r_T\,\mathrm du\,\mathrm dv\,\mathrm d^2\theta.$$* *For each fixed finite $T$ and each $\eta_{\rm map}>0$, these observations persist for all data in a sufficiently small relative weighted smooth neighborhood of $d_T$, with signal lower bound $A_T$ in place of $2A_T$. The persisted common coordinates need not be optical. The canonical section volume, injective maps into $O$, and frame and density bounds persist. Their backward maps satisfy $$\sup_{v\in T+J_0}\\|\widehat\Psi_{T,v}-\Psi_{T,v}\\|_{C^1} <\eta_{\rm map}, \qquad \sup_{v\in T+J_0}\\|\widehat\Psi_{T,v}-\operatorname{incl}\\|_{C^1} \le\delta_T+\eta_{\rm map}.$$ The neighborhood may depend on $T$ and $\eta_{\rm map}$.
  - Explanation: Identical numerical terminal states induce injective backward seed maps converging to inclusion and preserving canonical section measure. Frame, density, signal, and flow-map bounds persist for each fixed finite experiment under sufficiently close data.
- **claim-base-frame-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-ho-base-comparison)): Suppose an extension chart has bounded metric and inverse metric, and the components of $U$ are bounded both in that chart and in $F$. Then the matrix $M$ with columns $F_i$ in chart coordinates and its inverse are bounded by a constant depending only on these bounds and <a href="#ho:gram-assumptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ho:gram-assumptions">[ho:gram-assumptions]</a>.
  - Explanation: A selected bounded unit timelike tangent, together with bounded chart and frame metrics and inverses, controls both frame-change matrices at the base point. Metric bounds alone would not control a Lorentz boost.
- **claim-loop-jets-and-curvature-coefficient** (bound, [source](#stmt-ho-loop-jets)): Uniformly in the observation positions and $X\in\mathcal X$, $$\label{ho:jet-bound} \sup_{0\le l\le17l_T}\\|\partial_l^{18}H_{x,X}(l)\\|\le Q_T.$$ The quadratic Taylor coefficient $C_2(x,X)$ of $H_{x,X}(l)-\mathrm{Id}$ is the matrix, in $F(x)$, of $\pm R(\partial_v,rX)$. Consequently $$\label{ho:curvature-cancellation} g\bigl(C_2(x,X)F_1,X\bigr) =\pm R(\partial_v,X,\partial_v,X)=\pm D(X,X).$$ The same curvature and loop-orientation convention fixes the signs.
  - Explanation: Rectangular loop holonomies have controlled high derivatives in loop size. Their quadratic coefficient recovers the null-angular curvature block, with cancellation of the frame rescaling factor.
- **claim-averaged-holonomy-obstruction** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-averaged-holonomy)): Assume <a href="#ho:gram-assumptions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ho:gram-assumptions">[ho:gram-assumptions]</a> on the observation box, bounded $F$ components of the selected unit tangents, and a fixed bounded momentum box in <a href="#ho:parameter-measure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ho:parameter-measure">[ho:parameter-measure]</a>. Fix one extension and one collection of at most $B$ charts with the metric, inverse-metric, and connection bounds in [def:exit-test](#def:exit-test). Let $\mathcal G_T\subset\mathcal P_T$ be the measurable parameters whose base point and unit tangent satisfy the margin and velocity conditions in at least one chart of this same collection. There is a measurable exceptional set $\mathcal N_T\subset\mathcal G_T$ such that $$\label{ho:exception-bound} \nu_T(\mathcal N_T)\le C e^{-\kappa T/16},$$ and, for all parameters in $\mathcal G_T\setminus\mathcal N_T$, $$\label{ho:D-small} \|D\|\le C e^{-13\kappa T/32}+Q_Te^{-\kappa T} =o(A_T).$$ The constants use only $B$ and the uniform observation bounds.
  - Explanation: Position and momentum averaging, followed by a stopped chart-transport argument and finite interpolation, makes the observed curvature block smaller than packet amplitude outside a vanishing exceptional parameter set.
- **claim-extension-tests-nowhere-dense** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-nowhere-dense)): For the single neighborhood $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$ fixed in [prop:bg-package](#prop:bg-package), every set $E(O,B)$ of [def:exit-test](#def:exit-test) is relatively nowhere dense in $\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$.
  - Explanation: Each existential extension test has closure with empty relative interior in the same fixed data neighborhood. A density assumption would yield matched observations with a persistent packet signal and incompatible averaged curvature bounds.

```json
      "exact_statement": "For every $d\\in\\mathcal U_{\\varepsilon}$, the comparison geometry has the following properties.*\n\n\n\n1.  *The metrics $\\gamma^{\\pm1}$ and $\\mathcal A^{\\pm1}$ are bounded in angular $H^6$, and $\\xi_i,a^{-1}\\partial_vb$ are bounded in angular $H^5$. There are nonnegative measurable functions $g_i:\\mathbb R\\to[0,\\infty)$ with $$\\label{bg:cell-sum}\n     \\sum_{n\\in\\mathbb Z}\\sup_{[n,n+1]}g_i<\\infty$$ such that $$\\label{bg:directional-low}\n     \\norm{\\chi_i}_{H^4}+\\norm{\\ell_i+\\kappa}_{H^4}\n           +\\norm{B_i}_{H^5}\n     \\le C\\bigl(q+g_i(y_i)\\bigr).$$ The corresponding first pure longitudinal derivatives $\\mathfrak L_i\\chi_i,L_i\\ell_i$, and $\\partial_u b$ in direction $0$, have directional summable bounds through angular $H^2$, after enlarging the $g_i$.*\n\n\n2.  *The first ordinary longitudinal jets satisfy $$\\label{bg:low-jets}\n    \\begin{gathered}\n     \\norm b_{H^5}+\\sum_i\\norm{(\\chi_i,\\ell_i+\\kappa)}_{H^4}\\to0,\\\\\n     \\sum_{k=0}^1\\left(\\norm{\\partial_{y_k}b}_{H^2}\n           +\\sum_i\\norm{\\partial_{y_k}(\\chi_i,\\ell_i)}_{H^2}\\right)\\to0,\\\\\n     \\sum_{k=0}^1\\left(\\norm{\\partial_{y_k}\\gamma}_{H^4}\n           +\\norm{\\partial_{y_k}\\log a+\\kappa}_{H^4}\\right)\\to0\n    \\end{gathered}$$ as $t\\to\\infty$ and $|u|,|v|\\to\\infty$. The displayed norms are bounded everywhere in the wedge.*\n\n\n3.  *For fixed $U\\in\\mathbb R$, $C>0$, and each fixed ordinary coordinate derivative order, the fields $$\\label{bg:high-fields}\n     \\gamma^{\\pm1},\\quad\\log\\mathcal A,\\quad b,\n           \\quad a^{-1}\\partial_vb$$ have $e^{o(T)}$ bounds on $$\\label{bg:truncated-wedge}\n     u\\le U,\\qquad v\\le CT,\\qquad t\\ge0.$$ The assertion holds for either optical ordering and on restricted coordinate-path segments. Fixed derivatives of $a$, $\\sqrt a$, and $\\partial_vb$ retain respectively the factors $a$, $\\sqrt a$, and $a$, with subexponential coefficient bounds.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bg-runout",
        "exact_quote": "Every seed whose future geodesic has finite maximal proper lifetime in $M_d$ crosses $\\mathcal S$. Its first runout from $\\mathcal P_d$ is either a branch, where one optical coordinate has a finite limit and the other tends to $+\\infty$, or a corner, where both tend to $+\\infty$. For each fixed datum, seeds whose first runout is a corner form a $\\mu_d$-null set.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bg-branch-tube",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a branch seed, with optical ordering $u\\to u_*\\in\\mathbb R$, $v\\to\\infty$. There is a relatively compact open neighborhood $\\mathcal N$ of this seed, as small as desired, with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *All seeds in $\\overline{\\mathcal N}$ have an initial compact flow tube from $\\Sigma$ to a fixed late $v$ section, followed by tracks contained in a common compact $u$ range and a sphere coordinate chart. The section hits in the earlier flow tube are transverse and single-valued. In canonical state variables $(u,\\theta,\\pi_u,P)$, both momenta are bounded and $$\\label{bg:p}\n     p=-(\\pi_u+b^AP_A)=a\\dot v$$ has positive upper and lower bounds. Each track has branch runout in this ordering.*\n\n2.  *For a sufficiently small fixed interval $J$ about zero and an unbounded sequence of $T$, there are product observation boxes $\\mathcal Q_T$ of fixed side lengths in $(u,v-T,\\theta)$ containing all hitting positions of these tracks at $v\\in T+J$. The closure of a fixed enlargement stays in the common $u$ and angular-chart margins. The angular feet on $\\mathcal S$ of the $L_0$ segments from every point of that enlarged closure lie, with a fixed interior margin, in one admissible small packet launch patch. The patch size is fixed by the low-order ray and collar construction, independently of later derivative and accuracy choices.*\n\n\n3.  *For fixed $u_2>u_*$, $c_2>0$, and sufficiently small $c_0>0$, the neighborhood, $J$, and box sizes can be chosen so that all entry-to-observation track portions and enlarged observation boxes fit with strict outer margins in $$\\label{bg:experiment-domain}\n    \\begin{gathered}\n     0\\le t\\le L_T=(T+u_*)/2+c_0,\\\\\n     u\\le u_2-c_0t/T,\n     \\qquad v\\le T+c_2-c_0t/T.\n    \\end{gathered}$$ The slice $x$ intervals have a positive lower length, uniformly for large $T$. No margin from the entry surface $t=0$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-ex-graph",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that a future timelike $C^1$ curve exits $M$ as in Definition <a href=\"#intro:extension\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"intro:extension\">1</a>. There is a precompact ambient coordinate chart with coordinates $(s,z)\\in\\mathbb R\\times\\mathbb R^3$, a constant $c>0$, a bottom height $-d_0<0$, a spatial ball $D$, and a Lipschitz function $f:D\\to\\mathbb R$, with Lipschitz constant $L<\\infty$, having the following properties:*\n\n1.  *$s$ is temporal, and every vector $(1,\\eta)$ with $|\\eta|<c$ is uniformly future timelike;*\n\n2.  *$(-d_0,z)\\in I_M^+(\\Sigma)$ for all $z\\in D$;*\n\n3.  *the vertical segment $\\{(s,z):-d_0\\le s<f(z)\\}$ lies in $M$, while $(f(z),z)\\in\\partial M$;*\n\n4.  *$f(0)=0$ and, for some $0<\\delta<d_0/4$, $-\\delta<f(z)\\le\\delta$. The rectangle containing these segments has an upper face above $2\\delta$.*\n\n*The metric, its inverse, the positive spatial block $(g_{ij})$, and the timelike normalization on any closed narrower cone have uniform bounds and uniform nondegeneracy on this rectangle.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ex-graph",

The rectangle containing these segments has an upper face above $2\\delta$.*\n\n*The metric, its inverse, the positive spatial block $(g_{ij})$, and the timelike normalization on any closed narrower cone have uniform bounds and uniform nondegeneracy on this rectangle.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-exit-family",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of [ex:graph](#ex:graph), there is a measurable set $S\\subset T\\Sigma$ of positive smooth section measure such that every future unit geodesic with seed in $S$ has finite maximal future proper lifetime in the full $M$ and an ambient endpoint on $\\partial M$. The endpoints lie in one compact subset of an ambient chart. Moreover, there are finitely many precompact ambient charts $\\psi_j:U_j\\to V_j\\subset\\mathbb R^4$ and constants $C<\\infty$, $m>0$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *on each entire $V_j$, the metric and inverse metric have component norms at most $C$, and $\\|\\widetilde\\Gamma_j\\|_{L^2(V_j)}\\le C$;*\n\n2.  *for every seed in $S$ and every proper time from its intersection with $\\Sigma$ to its full future endpoint, excluding only the endpoint itself, at least one $j$ satisfies $$\\label{ex:chart-witness}\n          \\operatorname{dist}\\bigl(\\psi_j(\\gamma(\\tau)),\n                                      \\mathbb R^4\\setminus V_j\\bigr)\\ge m,\n          \\qquad\n          |(\\psi_j)_*\\dot\\gamma(\\tau)|\\le C.$$*\n\n*Here the section measure is the pullback of canonical volume on $T^*\\Sigma$ under $w\\mapsto h(w,\\cdot)$, where $h$ is the induced metric. In particular it has the same null sets as every smooth positive measure on $T\\Sigma$.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-exit-family",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of [ex:graph](#ex:graph), there is a measurable set $S\\subset T\\Sigma$ of positive smooth section measure such that every future unit geodesic with seed in $S$ has finite maximal future proper lifetime in the full $M$ and an ambient endpoint on $\\partial M$. The endpoints lie in one compact subset of an ambient chart. Moreover, there are finitely many precompact ambient charts $\\psi_j:U_j\\to V_j\\subset\\mathbb R^4$ and constants $C<\\infty$, $m>0$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *on each entire $V_j$, the metric and inverse metric have component norms at most $C$, and $\\|\\widetilde\\Gamma_j\\|_{L^2(V_j)}\\le C$;*\n\n2.  *for every seed in $S$ and every proper time from its intersection with $\\Sigma$ to its full future endpoint, excluding only the endpoint itself, at least one $j$ satisfies $$\\label{ex:chart-witness}\n          \\operatorname{dist}\\bigl(\\psi_j(\\gamma(\\tau)),\n                                      \\mathbb R^4\\setminus V_j\\bigr)\\ge m,\n          \\qquad\n          |(\\psi_j)_*\\dot\\gamma(\\tau)|\\le C.$$*\n\n*Here the section measure is the pullback of canonical volume on $T^*\\Sigma$ under $w\\mapsto h(w,\\cdot)$, where $h$ is the induced metric.       "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of [ex:graph](#ex:graph), there is a measurable set $S\\subset T\\Sigma$ of positive smooth section measure such that every future unit geodesic with seed in $S$ has finite maximal future proper lifetime in the full $M$ and an ambient endpoint on $\\partial M$. The endpoints lie in one compact subset of an ambient chart. Moreover, there are finitely many precompact ambient charts $\\psi_j:U_j\\to V_j\\subset\\mathbb R^4$ and constants $C<\\infty$, $m>0$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *on each entire $V_j$, the metric and inverse metric have component norms at most $C$, and $\\|\\widetilde\\Gamma_j\\|_{L^2(V_j)}\\le C$;*\n\n2.  *for every seed in $S$ and every proper time from its intersection with $\\Sigma$ to its full future endpoint, excluding only the endpoint itself, at least one $j$ satisfies $$\\label{ex:chart-witness}\n          \\operatorname{dist}\\bigl(\\psi_j(\\gamma(\\tau)),\n                                      \\mathbb R^4\\setminus V_j\\bigr)\\ge m,\n          \\qquad\n          |(\\psi_j)_*\\dot\\gamma(\\tau)|\\le C.$$*\n\n*Here the section measure is the pullback of canonical volume on $T^*\\Sigma$ under $w\\mapsto h(w,\\cdot)$, where $h$ is the induced metric. In particular it has the same null sets as every smooth positive measure on $T\\Sigma$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-packet-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite $m\\ge10$, finite jet budget $N$, positive loss tolerance $\\eta_{\\rm out}$, and $0<\\nu<1$, one can choose $\\zeta>0$ and finite construction orders so that the following objects exist for arbitrarily large $T$ along the indicated sequence.*\n\n1.  *There are exact smooth complete vacuum data $d_T\\in\\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge with $p_m(d_T-d)\\to0$. They agree with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and an expanding protected ball on the selected end. The permitted exterior correction tail is retained.*\n\n\n\n2.  *An exact exterior evolution of $d_T$ reaches the required part of $\\mathcal S$, with overlapping collars and strict outflow margins. In the fixed background wave-map description its entry spacetime jets differ from $g+h^p$ by $O(A_T^2e^{\\eta_{\\rm out} T})$. Here $$\\label{pk:packet}\n     h^p=A_T\\,\\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\\operatorname{Re}\n           \\left(e^{i\\lambda v}\\sum_{j=0}^{J_*}\\lambda^{-j}p_j\\right).$$ The leading coefficient is real, trace-free, and annihilates $L_0$. On the entire observation box $\\mathcal Q_T$, $$\\label{pk:screen-lower}\n     |(p_0)_{AB}|_\\gamma\\ge e^{-o(T)}.$$ At every fixed required base order the ordinary mixed coordinate jets of the normalized amplitude components are $e^{o(T)}$, and the linearized Ricci and gauge errors have arbitrarily high inverse-frequency accuracy.*\n\n3.  *There is a metric $\\bar g$ exactly of double-null form, with fields $\\bar\\gamma,\\log\\bar{\\mathcal A},\\bar b$ and their exact geometric derived fields. Through order $N$, its differences from the background fields, including $q^{-1}\\partial_vb$, are $O(A_Te^{\\eta_{\\rm out} T})$. Its defects in the null structure and angular equations <a href=\"#dn:D2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dn:D2\">[dn:D2]</a>–<a href=\"#dn:D3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dn:D3\">[dn:D3]</a> are $O(A_T^2e^{\\eta_{\\rm out} T})$. If the actual exterior induced data are written in double-null coordinates initialized by the background values on $\\mathcal S$, their full entry jets differ from this template by the same quadratic bound.*\n\n*All entry discrepancies and all template changes and defects vanish for $v<(1-\\nu)T$. Any additional finite initial or residual jet buffers may be included in $N$. Positive frequency powers at these base orders are independent of the later orders used to improve linear accuracy. At the finitely many high entry orders ultimately requested by [thm:dn-comparison](#thm:dn-comparison), the actual fields have $e^{o(T)}$ bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z=(z^0,z^1,z^\\theta)$ be the variations of the coordinate functions, with $Z=0$ on $\\mathcal S$, for the input $h^p$. In unnormalized components $h^p_{ij}=h^p(L_i,L_j)$ and $h^p_{iA}=h^p(L_i,\\partial_A)$, they obey $$\\label{pk:linear-optical}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\partial_vz^0&=-\\frac{h^p_{11}}{2a},&\n L_0z^1&=-\\frac{h^p_{00}}{2a},\\\\\n \\partial_vz^\\theta\n &=b\\partial_vz^0+a\\gamma^{-1}\\mathrm d_\\theta z^0\n                         +\\gamma^{-1}h^p_{1\\cdot}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ For every fixed number of angular derivatives and $r$ total longitudinal derivatives, $$\\label{pk:coordinate-gain}\n |\\partial_{u,v}^{r}\\partial_\\theta^sZ|\n                      \\le A_T\\lambda^{r-1}e^{o(T)}.$$ The fixed-new-label metric variation is $k=h^p-\\mathcal L_Zg$. Its field variations $k_\\gamma,k_H,k_b$, where $H=\\log\\mathcal A$, and the relative derivative $q^{-1}\\partial_vk_b$ satisfy $$\\label{pk:relative-linear}\n \\|(k_\\gamma,k_H,k_b,q^{-1}\\partial_vk_b)\\|_{C^s}\n                        \\le A_T\\lambda^{P_s}e^{o(T)}$$ at every fixed required order. The exponents $P_s$ depend only on that order, not on subsequent inverse-accuracy choices.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge",

Its field variations $k_\\gamma,k_H,k_b$, where $H=\\log\\mathcal A$, and the relative derivative $q^{-1}\\partial_vk_b$ satisfy $$\\label{pk:relative-linear}\n \\|(k_\\gamma,k_H,k_b,q^{-1}\\partial_vk_b)\\|_{C^s}\n                        \\le A_T\\lambda^{P_s}e^{o(T)}$$ at every fixed required order.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-dn-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed background and branch tube in [lem:bg-branch-tube](#lem:bg-branch-tube), every finite $m,s$, and every sufficiently small fixed $c>0$, the parameters in [prop:packet-preparation](#prop:packet-preparation) can be chosen so that its exact entry data have a smooth vacuum development on [dn:domain](#dn:domain) in the double-null gauge initialized there. The relative lapse and the sphere metric remain positive and uniformly nondegenerate. For $$\\mathcal U=(\\gamma,\\log\\mathcal A,b,\\chi_0,\\chi_1,\n                       \\ell_0,\\ell_1,\\xi_0,\\xi_1)$$ one has, in a fixed sphere atlas and ordinary coordinate derivatives, $$\\label{dn:main-error}\n \\max_{|I|\\le s}\\|\\partial^I(\\mathcal U'-\\bar{\\mathcal U})\n                         \\|_{L^\\infty(\\Omega_T)}\n       \\le e^{-(1+c)\\alpha T}$$ for all sufficiently large $T$ in the selected experiment sequence. The same conclusion holds for $q^{-1}\\partial_vb'-q^{-1}\\partial_v\\bar b$ at any preassigned finite order, after increasing the finite order budget. The individual ordinary jets of the primed relative fields through every order used in the experiment are $e^{o(T)}$, uniformly for this moving family. The exact solution and the template equal the background where $v<(1-\\nu)T$. The exact solution agrees on an entry collar, by local geometric uniqueness, with the actual exterior solution of [prop:packet-preparation](#prop:packet-preparation).",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-dn-high-uniform",
        "exact_quote": "Assume [dn:bootstrap](#dn:bootstrap) on an existing initial partial slab $\\Omega_T\\cap\\{0\\le t\\le t_*\\}$ of [dn:domain](#dn:domain), with the same outer margins. For every fixed finite order $n$ there is a finite initial order $r(n)$ with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-dn-low-error",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\eta_2>0$, the input loss $\\eta$ and the support fraction $\\nu$ may be chosen sufficiently small, with the finite base orders fixed, so that on every existing bootstrap subslab $$\\label{dn:low-conclusion}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le L_T}\n \\| (\\Delta\\gamma,\\Delta\\log\\mathcal A,\\Delta b,\n                   \\Delta\\chi_i,\\Delta\\ell_i,\\Delta\\xi_i)\n                         \\|_{L^2(I_T(t)\\times\\mathbb S^2)}\n \\le A_T^2e^{\\eta_2T}.$$ The supremum is restricted to available slices. At the same times, $$\\label{dn:low-auxiliary-conclusion}\n \\sum_i\\|Y_i+Z_i+z_i\\|_{L^2(I_T(t))}\n +\\|(\\nabla\\Delta\\gamma,\\nabla\\Delta b)\n                  \\|_{L^2(I_T(t)\\times\\mathbb S^2)}\n \\le A_T^2e^{\\eta_2T}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-matched-seeds",
        "exact_quote": "For $v\\in T+J_0$, use the same numerical states <a href=\"#at:terminal-state\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"at:terminal-state\">[at:terminal-state]</a> in $g_T$ on its future unit shell. Their backward geodesics give maps $\\Psi_{T,v}:O_0\\to O$ with $$\\label{at:seed-closeness}\n \\sup_{v\\in T+J_0}\\|\\Psi_{T,v}-\\operatorname{incl}\\|_{C^1}\n                                  \\longrightarrow0.$$ Write $\\delta_T$ for the left side of <a href=\"#at:seed-closeness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"at:seed-closeness\">[at:seed-closeness]</a>. Each map is injective and preserves the corresponding section measure: $\\Psi_{T,v}^*\\mu_{d_T}=\\mu_d$ on $O_0$. In particular $\\mathcal H_v(O_0)$ has canonical volume $\\mu_d(O_0)$ for every such $v$.*\n\n\n*Put $$\\label{at:frame}\n r_T=e^{-\\kappa T},\\qquad\n F=(\\partial_u,r_T^{-1}\\partial_v,\n                    \\partial_{\\theta^1},\\partial_{\\theta^2}).$$ For $g$ and $g_T$ on the larger observation box, the Gram matrices in $F$ and their inverses are uniformly bounded and nondegenerate. Their ordinary coordinate jets through order thirty are $e^{o(T)}$. The selected unit tangent is bounded in $F$, all state momenta lie in one bounded box, and $$\\label{at:volume}\n c r_T\\,\\mathrm du\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm d^2\\theta\n \\le \\mathrm d\\operatorname{vol}_g,\\ \\mathrm d\\operatorname{vol}_{g_T}\n \\le C r_T\\,\\mathrm du\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm d^2\\theta.$$*\n\n*For each fixed finite $T$ and each $\\eta_{\\rm map}>0$, these observations persist for all data in a sufficiently small relative weighted smooth neighborhood of $d_T$, with signal lower bound $A_T$ in place of $2A_T$. The persisted common coordinates need not be optical. The canonical section volume, injective maps into $O$, and frame and density bounds persist. Their backward maps satisfy $$\\sup_{v\\in T+J_0}\\|\\widehat\\Psi_{T,v}-\\Psi_{T,v}\\|_{C^1}\n <\\eta_{\\rm map},\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{v\\in T+J_0}\\|\\widehat\\Psi_{T,v}-\\operatorname{incl}\\|_{C^1}\n \\le\\delta_T+\\eta_{\\rm map}.$$ The neighborhood may depend on $T$ and $\\eta_{\\rm map}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-matched-seeds",

      "exact_statement": "For $v\\in T+J_0$, use the same numerical states <a href=\"#at:terminal-state\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"at:terminal-state\">[at:terminal-state]</a> in $g_T$ on its future unit shell.         "source_id": "PM-264.1"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ho-loop-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly in the observation positions and $X\\in\\mathcal X$, $$\\label{ho:jet-bound}\n \\sup_{0\\le l\\le17l_T}\\|\\partial_l^{18}H_{x,X}(l)\\|\\le Q_T.$$ The quadratic Taylor coefficient $C_2(x,X)$ of $H_{x,X}(l)-\\mathrm{Id}$ is the matrix, in $F(x)$, of $\\pm R(\\partial_v,rX)$. Consequently $$\\label{ho:curvature-cancellation}\n g\\bigl(C_2(x,X)F_1,X\\bigr)\n       =\\pm R(\\partial_v,X,\\partial_v,X)=\\pm D(X,X).$$ The same curvature and loop-orientation convention fixes the signs.",
        "source_id": "PM-264.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ho-loop-jets",
          "exact_quote": "Uniformly in the observation positions and $X\\in\\mathcal X$, $$\\label{ho:jet-bound}\n \\sup_{0\\le l\\le17l_T}\\|\\partial_l^{18}H_{x,X}(l)\\|\\le Q_T.$$ The quadratic Taylor coefficient $C_2(x,X)$ of $H_{x,X}(l)-\\mathrm{Id}$ is the matrix, in $F(x)$, of $\\pm R(\\partial_v,rX)$. Consequently $$\\label{ho:curvature-cancellation}\n g\\bigl(C_2(x,X)F_1,X\\bigr)\n       =\\pm R(\\partial_v,X,\\partial_v,X)=\\pm D(X,X).$$ The same curvature and loop-orientation convention fixes the signs.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Uniformly in the observation positions and $X\\in\\mathcal X$, $$\\label{ho:jet-bound}\n \\sup_{0\\le l\\le17l_T}\\|\\partial_l^{18}H_{x,X}(l)\\|\\le Q_T.$$ The quadratic Taylor coefficient $C_2(x,X)$ of $H_{x,X}(l)-\\mathrm{Id}$ is the matrix, in $F(x)$, of $\\pm R(\\partial_v,rX)$. Consequently $$\\label{ho:curvature-cancellation}\n g\\bigl(C_2(x,X)F_1,X\\bigr)\n       =\\pm R(\\partial_v,X,\\partial_v,X)=\\pm D(X,X).$$ The same curvature and loop-orientation convention fixes the signs.",
      "explanation": "Rectangular loop holonomies have controlled high derivatives in loop size. Their quadratic coefficient recovers the null-angular curvature block, with cancellation of the frame rescaling factor.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Future weak extension** ([source](#stmt-intro-extension)): A future $C^0\cap W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ extension of $(M_d,g_d)$ consists of a connected time-oriented smooth four-manifold $\widetilde M$, a continuous nondegenerate Lorentzian metric $\widetilde g$ of signature $(-,+,+,+)$ whose components belong to $W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$ in smooth charts, and a smooth time-orientation-preserving isometric embedding $\iota:M_d\hookrightarrow\widetilde M$ with proper open image. There must also be a future-directed timelike $C^1$ curve $c:[0,1]\to\widetilde M$ such that $$c([0,1))\subset\iota(M_d), \qquad c(1)\in\partial\iota(M_d).$$ No field equation or global-hyperbolicity condition is imposed outside the image.
- **Fixed-order subexponential bounds** ([source](#stmt-conv-bg-subexp)): For a fixed datum and fixed finite derivative orders, a bound $Q_T\le e^{o(T)}$ means that for every $\delta>0$ there are $C_\delta,T_\delta$ such that $Q_T\le C_\delta e^{\delta T}$ for $T\ge T_\delta$. For a positive quantity, a two-sided bound also bounds its reciprocal. Constants can depend on the datum and the chosen orders. Uniformity for a family of changing data is asserted only when proved separately.
- **Existential extension tests** ([source](#stmt-def-exit-test)): For $O\in\mathcal B$ and $B\in\mathbb N$, $B\ge1$, let $E(O,B)\subset\mathcal U_{\varepsilon}$ consist of the data satisfying both of the following requirements. 1. There is an extension of $M_d$ of the regularity and type specified in Definition <a href="#intro:extension" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="intro:extension">1</a>, and a measurable $S\subset O$ with $$\label{ex:density-test} \mu_d(S)>.99\,\mu_d(O),$$ such that every seed in $S$ has finite full future lifetime. There are at most $B$ precompact ambient charts $\psi_j:U_j\to V_j$ witnessing [ex:chart-witness](#ex:chart-witness) along each entire such geodesic, from $\Sigma$ to its full endpoint. On the whole of every $V_j$, each of the metric norm, inverse metric norm, and Christoffel $L^2$ norm is at most $B$. At each point a selected chart has velocity norm at most $B$ and Euclidean distance to $\mathbb R^4\setminus V_j$ at least $1/B$. 2. There is an observation neighborhood $\mathcal N$ satisfying all three parts of [lem:bg-branch-tube](#lem:bg-branch-tube) for this datum, with $\overline O\subset\mathcal N$ and the optical ordering chosen so that $v\to\infty$. One choice of its central branch track, compact state ranges, positive $p$ bounds, transverse earlier flow tube, interval $J$, observation boxes, admissible launch patch, unbounded sequence of $T$, and constants $u_2,c_2,c_0$ serves every seed in $\overline{\mathcal N}$. In particular, the entry-to-observation portions fit <a href="#bg:experiment-domain" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bg:experiment-domain">[bg:experiment-domain]</a> with its outer margins and positive slice-length bound. The admissible patch has the low-order ray and collar properties specified before [lem:bg-branch-tube](#lem:bg-branch-tube). All tube constants, charts, patches, and subsequences in this definition are existential witnesses for the given datum. They are not additional countable indices and are not required to work in a neighborhood of that datum.
- **Canonical seed measure** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Identify $T\Sigma$ with $T^*\Sigma$ by $w\mapsto h(w,\cdot)$, and let $\mu_d$ be the pullback of its canonical symplectic volume.
- **Admissible packet launch patch** ([source](#pm-h-10)): We call it admissible when the covectors $\mathrm dv$ have backward null rays crossing the entry, horizon and original initial hypersurfaces transversely, escaping into the selected exterior end, with uniformly nondegenerate geometry on the fixed-depth entry collar.
- **Double-null template metric** ([source](#pk:template)): Define the template metric by the following exact assignments: $$\label{pk:template} \begin{gathered} \bar\gamma=\gamma+k_\gamma,\qquad \bar{\mathcal A}=\mathcal A e^{k_H},\qquad \bar a=q\bar{\mathcal A},\qquad \bar b=b+k_b,\\ \bar g=-2\bar a\,\mathrm du\,\mathrm dv+ \bar\gamma_{AB}(\mathrm d\theta^A-\bar b^A\mathrm du) (\mathrm d\theta^B-\bar b^B\mathrm du). \end{gathered}$$
- **Observed curvature block** ([source](#pm-h-32)): Adopt $R(X,Y)=[\nabla_X,\nabla_Y]-\nabla_{[X,Y]}$ and set $$\label{at:D-definition} D(g)_{AB}=g\bigl(R(\partial_v,\partial_A)\partial_v, \partial_B\bigr).$$
- **Canonical observation parameter measure** ([source](#ho:parameter-measure)): The full parameter set and its measure are $$\label{ho:parameter-measure} \mathcal P_T=\{(v,u,\theta,\pi_u,P):v\in T+J_0, \ (u,\theta,\pi_u,P)\in\mathcal P_{T,v}\},\qquad \mathrm d\nu_T=\mathrm dv\,\mathrm du\,\mathrm d^2\theta\,\mathrm d\pi_u\,\mathrm d^2P.$$

| q-packet-amplitude | A_T | = | $e^{-\alpha T}$ | Late observation times follow the chosen unbounded sequence. |
| q-packet-frequency | \lambda | = | $e^{\zeta T}$ | Frequency growth is selected after finite base orders and tolerances, before increasing inverse-frequency accuracy. |

| q-quadratic-slice-error | Actual-minus-template slice error | ≤ | $A_T^2e^{\eta_2T}$ | Input loss and support fraction are chosen sufficiently small for the desired output loss. |
| q-whole-patch-curvature-contrast | \|D(g_T)-D(g)\| | > | $2A_T$ | The chosen experiment sequence and cosine-good observations. |
| q-observation-frame-scale | r_T | = | $e^{-\kappa T}$ | The scale is constant throughout each fixed observation box. |
| q-holonomy-loop-scale | l_T | = | $e^{-\kappa T/16}$ | Loop sizes are sampled at the fixed interpolation nodes. |
| q-holonomy-exceptional-measure | \nu_T(\mathcal N_T) | ≤ | $C e^{-\kappa T/16}$ | Constants depend on chart bounds and uniform observation bounds. |
| q-averaged-curvature-smallness | \|D\| | ≤ | $C e^{-13\kappa T/32}+Q_Te^{-\kappa T}$ | Fixed derivative budgets and subexponential coefficient bounds. |

```json
            "anchor": "ho:gram-assumptions",
            "exact_quote": "The following argument applies separately to the background metric and to any persisted finite experiment satisfying [prop:matched-seeds](#prop:matched-seeds). Denote either metric temporarily by $g$ and put $G_{ij}=g(F_i,F_j)$. On an observation box containing the positions in <a href=\"#ho:parameter-measure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ho:parameter-measure\">[ho:parameter-measure]</a> with a fixed margin, we have $$\\label{ho:gram-assumptions}\n \\|G\\|+\\|G^{-1}\\|\\le C,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{|\\beta|\\le30}\n \\bigl(\\|\\partial^\\beta G\\|+\\|\\partial^\\beta G^{-1}\\|\\bigr)\n       \\le Q_T,\\qquad Q_T=e^{o(T)}.$$ All derivatives here are ordinary coordinate derivatives. <a id="pm-s3"></a>
```
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-background-package** [claim](#stmt-prop-bg-package) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bg-package): Import the stated comparison interfaces and enlarge directional envelopes to include required pure jets. -> Low geometry and fixed-order subexponential coefficient bounds.
- **step-runout-classification** [claim](#stmt-prop-bg-runout) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bg-runout): Combine finite-full-lifetime classification with canonical section-volume corner removal. -> Branch runout for almost every finite-lifetime seed.
- **step-branch-tube** [claim](#stmt-lem-bg-branch-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bg-branch-tube): Use the integrable reduced Hamiltonian field, Gronwall estimates, and launch-foot variational bounds to obtain uniform observation margins. -> Open seed tube, whole-footprint launch patch, and finite observation domains.
- **step-intrinsic-homotopy** [claim](#stmt-ex-homotopy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-homotopy): Show the set of interior homotopy members is open and closed using a compact intrinsic causal diamond. -> The entire causal homotopy remains interior.
- **step-first-exit-graph** [claim](#stmt-ex-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-graph): Choose a straight timelike first exit and rule out excessive graph slope by fixed-endpoint timelike deformations. -> A local Lipschitz first-exit graph with uniform algebraic bounds.
- **step-connection-boxes** [claim](#stmt-ex-boxes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ex-boxes): Average connection mass over graph-centered boxes and use absolute continuity on a shrinking layer. -> Shrinking boxes with vanishing normalized connection mass.
- **step-positive-exit-family** [claim](#stmt-prop-exit-family) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exit-family): Preserve canonical stopped-flow volume, average connection integrals, exclude slope stops, and deform any surviving arc through the boundary. -> A positive-measure family with finite full lifetime and uniform chart witnesses.
- **step-countable-exit-cover** [claim](#stmt-prop-exit-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exit-cover): Remove corner seeds, choose a branch density point, and select a fine basis ball with a sufficiently large integer chart bound. -> A countable cover by existential extension tests.
- **step-linear-optical-gauge** [claim](#stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge): Linearize eikonal and angle equations, use oscillatory integration, then estimate torsion before recovering the relative shift derivative algebraically. -> Controlled linear double-null field changes and relative shift derivative.
- **step-packet-preparation** [claim](#stmt-prop-packet-preparation) / [proof](#pk:template-defect): Transport polarization across the whole footprint, transfer constrained beams, correct exterior constraints while preserving the protected ball, propagate actual exterior data, and construct the exact template. -> Exact full-bridge data, actual entry jets, and a template with quadratic defects.
- **step-uniform-high-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-dn-high-uniform) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dn-high-uniform): Control weighted angular currents, recover unweighted fields by transport, and induct on longitudinal jets with finite angular buffers. -> Uniform fixed-order subexponential ordinary jets without uncontrolled high-norm constants.
- **step-quadratic-low-error** [claim](#stmt-prop-dn-low-error) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dn-low-error): Subtract using actual transport operators, retain lapse compensation in angular currents, rescale one-way limiting couplings, and use late support to shorten the bad-rate strip. -> Quadratic unweighted slice error with arbitrarily small exponential loss.
- **step-double-null-continuation** [claim](#stmt-thm-dn-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-dn-low-error-2): Interpolate low error with high bounds, recover time derivatives without inverse lapse division, improve the bootstrap, and restart at fixed finite experiment time. -> The exact vacuum experiment reaches the whole finite domain with the required finite-jet comparison.
- **step-whole-bridge-attachment** [claim](#stmt-prop-experiment-attachment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-experiment-attachment): Identify actual matching data on collars, retain past-preserved cuts and the entire bridge, prove the assembled region is globally hyperbolic, and invoke maximal-development uniqueness. -> Finite experiments embedded in the full maximal development.
- **step-curvature-signal** [claim](#stmt-prop-curvature-signal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-curvature-signal): Differentiate the leading oscillation in the shift-free curvature formula and dominate linear lower-order and quadratic errors using whole-footprint polarization. -> Relative curvature contrast throughout the cosine-good observation patch.
- **step-matched-seeds** [claim](#stmt-prop-matched-seeds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-matched-seeds): Match canonical terminal states, compare backward Hamiltonian flows, preserve transverse section volume, and apply compact finite Cauchy stability. -> Injective measure-preserving matched seed maps and persisted finite observations.
- **step-base-frame-control** [claim](#stmt-ho-base-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ho-base-comparison): Convert the Lorentzian forms to positive definite forms using the selected unit timelike tangent and compare them in both bases. -> Uniform base frame-change and inverse bounds.
- **step-loop-jets** [claim](#stmt-ho-loop-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ho-loop-jets): Contract Koszul coefficients with scaled loop directions, differentiate edge transport equations, and identify the quadratic coefficient in a frame with vanishing base connection. -> A controlled interpolation remainder and curvature recovery from the quadratic coefficient.
- **step-averaged-holonomy** [claim](#stmt-prop-averaged-holonomy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-averaged-holonomy): Average translated stopped edges before estimating frame transport, remove exceptional parameters, improve both stop thresholds, and extract the quadratic holonomy coefficient by fixed interpolation. -> Curvature smaller than packet amplitude outside a vanishing exceptional set.
- **step-nowhere-dense-tests** [claim](#stmt-thm-nowhere-dense) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-nowhere-dense): Assume density, build late packets inside a finite-seminorm neighborhood, choose a persisted extendible comparison datum, and intersect the matched good families with the cosine-good observations. -> A contradiction to density of each existential extension test.
- **step-residual-inextendibility** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-nowhere-dense-2): Combine the countable cover with relative nowhere denseness and pull back the fixed-center normalization. -> Meagreness of extendible data and a residual subset of future-inextendible data.

```json
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-exit-family",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of [ex:graph](#ex:graph), there is a measurable set $S\\subset T\\Sigma$ of positive smooth section measure such that every future unit geodesic with seed in $S$ has finite maximal future proper lifetime in the full $M$ and an ambient endpoint on $\\partial M$. The endpoints lie in one compact subset of an ambient chart. Moreover, there are finitely many precompact ambient charts $\\psi_j:U_j\\to V_j\\subset\\mathbb R^4$ and constants $C<\\infty$, $m>0$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *on each entire $V_j$, the metric and inverse metric have component norms at most $C$, and $\\|\\widetilde\\Gamma_j\\|_{L^2(V_j)}\\le C$;*\n\n2.  *for every seed in $S$ and every proper time from its intersection with $\\Sigma$ to its full future endpoint, excluding only the endpoint itself, at least one $j$ satisfies $$\\label{ex:chart-witness}\n          \\operatorname{dist}\\bigl(\\psi_j(\\gamma(\\tau)),\n                                      \\mathbb R^4\\setminus V_j\\bigr)\\ge m,\n          \\qquad\n          |(\\psi_j)_*\\dot\\gamma(\\tau)|\\le C.$$*\n\n*Here the section measure is the pullback of canonical volume on $T^*\\Sigma$ under $w\\mapsto h(w,\\cdot)$, where $h$ is the induced metric. In particular it has the same null sets as every smooth positive measure on $T\\Sigma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.1"
        }
      ],

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z=(z^0,z^1,z^\\theta)$ be the variations of the coordinate functions, with $Z=0$ on $\\mathcal S$, for the input $h^p$. In unnormalized components $h^p_{ij}=h^p(L_i,L_j)$ and $h^p_{iA}=h^p(L_i,\\partial_A)$, they obey $$\\label{pk:linear-optical}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\partial_vz^0&=-\\frac{h^p_{11}}{2a},&\n L_0z^1&=-\\frac{h^p_{00}}{2a},\\\\\n \\partial_vz^\\theta\n &=b\\partial_vz^0+a\\gamma^{-1}\\mathrm d_\\theta z^0\n                         +\\gamma^{-1}h^p_{1\\cdot}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ For every fixed number of angular derivatives and $r$ total longitudinal derivatives, $$\\label{pk:coordinate-gain}\n |\\partial_{u,v}^{r}\\partial_\\theta^sZ|\n                      \\le A_T\\lambda^{r-1}e^{o(T)}.$$ The fixed-new-label metric variation is $k=h^p-\\mathcal L_Zg$. Its field variations $k_\\gamma,k_H,k_b$, where $H=\\log\\mathcal A$, and the relative derivative $q^{-1}\\partial_vk_b$ satisfy $$\\label{pk:relative-linear}\n \\|(k_\\gamma,k_H,k_b,q^{-1}\\partial_vk_b)\\|_{C^s}\n                        \\le A_T\\lambda^{P_s}e^{o(T)}$$ at every fixed required order. The exponents $P_s$ depend only on that order, not on subsequent inverse-accuracy choices.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-linear-optical-gauge",
      "inputs": [
        "Constrained linear packet",
        "Normalized coefficient bounds",
        "Background optical entry values"
      ],
      "method": "Linearize eikonal and angle equations, use oscillatory integration, then estimate torsion before recovering the relative shift derivative algebraically.",
      "output": "Controlled linear double-null field changes and relative shift derivative.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-packet-linear-gauge"

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-packet-preparation",
          "exact_quote": "For every finite $m\\ge10$, finite jet budget $N$, positive loss tolerance $\\eta_{\\rm out}$, and $0<\\nu<1$, one can choose $\\zeta>0$ and finite construction orders so that the following objects exist for arbitrarily large $T$ along the indicated sequence.*\n\n1.  *There are exact smooth complete vacuum data $d_T\\in\\mathcal D$ on the whole bridge with $p_m(d_T-d)\\to0$. They agree with $d$ on the other end, the connecting region, and an expanding protected ball on the selected end. The permitted exterior correction tail is retained.*\n\n\n\n2.  *An exact exterior evolution of $d_T$ reaches the required part of $\\mathcal S$, with overlapping collars and strict outflow margins. In the fixed background wave-map description its entry spacetime jets differ from $g+h^p$ by $O(A_T^2e^{\\eta_{\\rm out} T})$. Here $$\\label{pk:packet}\n     h^p=A_T\\,\\operatorname{tr.rev.}_g\\operatorname{Re}\n           \\left(e^{i\\lambda v}\\sum_{j=0}^{J_*}\\lambda^{-j}p_j\\right).$$ The leading coefficient is real, trace-free, and annihilates $L_0$. On the entire observation box $\\mathcal Q_T$, $$\\label{pk:screen-lower}\n     |(p_0)_{AB}|_\\gamma\\ge e^{-o(T)}.$$ At every fixed required base order the ordinary mixed coordinate jets of the normalized amplitude components are $e^{o(T)}$, and the linearized Ricci and gauge errors have arbitrarily high inverse-frequency accuracy.*\n\n3.  *There is a metric $\\bar g$ exactly of double-null form, with fields $\\bar\\gamma,\\log\\bar{\\mathcal A},\\bar b$ and their exact geometric derived fields. Through order $N$, its differences from the background fields, including $q^{-1}\\partial_vb$, are $O(A_Te^{\\eta_{\\rm out} T})$. Its defects in the null structure and angular equations <a href=\"#dn:D2\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dn:D2\">[dn:D2]</a>–<a href=\"#dn:D3\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"dn:D3\">[dn:D3]</a> are $O(A_T^2e^{\\eta_{\\rm out} T})$. If the actual exterior induced data are written in double-null coordinates initialized by the background values on $\\mathcal S$, their full entry jets differ from this template by the same quadratic bound.*\n\n*All entry discrepancies and all template changes and defects vanish for $v<(1-\\nu)T$. Any additional finite initial or residual jet buffers may be included in $N$. Positive frequency powers at these base orders are independent of the later orders used to improve linear accuracy. At the finitely many high entry orders ultimately requested by [thm:dn-comparison](#thm:dn-comparison), the actual fields have $e^{o(T)}$ bounds.",
          "source_id": "PM-264.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-dn-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "For every fixed background and branch tube in [lem:bg-branch-tube](#lem:bg-branch-tube), every finite $m,s$, and every sufficiently small fixed $c>0$, the parameters in [prop:packet-preparation](#prop:packet-preparation) can be chosen so that its exact entry data have a smooth vacuum development on [dn:domain](#dn:domain) in the double-null gauge initialized there. The relative lapse and the sphere metric remain positive and uniformly nondegenerate. For $$\\mathcal U=(\\gamma,\\log\\mathcal A,b,\\chi_0,\\chi_1,\n                       \\ell_0,\\ell_1,\\xi_0,\\xi_1)$$ one has, in a fixed sphere atlas and ordinary coordinate derivatives, $$\\label{dn:main-error}\n \\max_{|I|\\le s}\\|\\partial^I(\\mathcal U'-\\bar{\\mathcal U})\n                         \\|_{L^\\infty(\\Omega_T)}\n       \\le e^{-(1+c)\\alpha T}$$ for all sufficiently large $T$ in the selected experiment sequence. The same conclusion holds for $q^{-1}\\partial_vb'-q^{-1}\\partial_v\\bar b$ at any preassigned finite order, after increasing the finite order budget. The individual ordinary jets of the primed relative fields through every order used in the experiment are $e^{o(T)}$, uniformly for this moving family. The exact solution and the template equal the background where $v<(1-\\nu)T$.           "anchor": "stmt-prop-matched-seeds",
          "exact_quote": "For $v\\in T+J_0$, use the same numerical states <a href=\"#at:terminal-state\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"at:terminal-state\">[at:terminal-state]</a> in $g_T$ on its future unit shell. Their backward geodesics give maps $\\Psi_{T,v}:O_0\\to O$ with $$\\label{at:seed-closeness}\n \\sup_{v\\in T+J_0}\\|\\Psi_{T,v}-\\operatorname{incl}\\|_{C^1}\n                                  \\longrightarrow0.$$ Write $\\delta_T$ for the left side of <a href=\"#at:seed-closeness\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"at:seed-closeness\">[at:seed-closeness]</a>. Each map is injective and preserves the corresponding section measure: $\\Psi_{T,v}^*\\mu_{d_T}=\\mu_d$ on $O_0$. In particular $\\mathcal H_v(O_0)$ has canonical volume $\\mu_d(O_0)$ for every such $v$.*\n\n\n*Put $$\\label{at:frame}\n r_T=e^{-\\kappa T},\\qquad\n F=(\\partial_u,r_T^{-1}\\partial_v,\n                    \\partial_{\\theta^1},\\partial_{\\theta^2}).$$ For $g$ and $g_T$ on the larger observation box, the Gram matrices in $F$ and their inverses are uniformly bounded and nondegenerate. Their ordinary coordinate jets through order thirty are $e^{o(T)}$. The selected unit tangent is bounded in $F$, all state momenta lie in one bounded box, and $$\\label{at:volume}\n c r_T\\,\\mathrm du\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm d^2\\theta\n \\le \\mathrm d\\operatorname{vol}_g,\\ \\mathrm d\\operatorname{vol}_{g_T}\n \\le C r_T\\,\\mathrm du\\,\\mathrm dv\\,\\mathrm d^2\\theta.$$*\n\n*For each fixed finite $T$ and each $\\eta_{\\rm map}>0$, these observations persist for all data in a sufficiently small relative weighted smooth neighborhood of $d_T$, with signal lower bound $A_T$ in place of $2A_T$. Their backward maps satisfy $$\\sup_{v\\in T+J_0}\\|\\widehat\\Psi_{T,v}-\\Psi_{T,v}\\|_{C^1}\n <\\eta_{\\rm map},\n \\qquad\n \\sup_{v\\in T+J_0}\\|\\widehat\\Psi_{T,v}-\\operatorname{incl}\\|_{C^1}\n \\le\\delta_T+\\eta_{\\rm map}.$$ The neighborhood may depend on $T$ and $\\eta_{\\rm map}$.",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-nowhere-dense-2"
    }
  ]
}
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-local-category-conclusion**: Future extensions with continuous nondegenerate metric and locally square-integrable weak connection occur only on a meagre set of admissible data near each fixed rotating subextremal Kerr bridge. Future-inextendible data contain a residual subset in the weighted smooth topology.
- **invariant-finite-relative-comparison**: The exact vacuum experiment remains positive and nondegenerate and approaches the double-null template more rapidly than the packet amplitude through every preassigned finite observation order. The comparison also controls the relative shift derivative.
- **invariant-canonical-matched-volume**: Matching numerical terminal positions and covariant momenta produces injective backward seed maps that preserve canonical section measure. This concerns phase-space hitting maps and does not require injectivity of their position projections.
- **invariant-relative-curvature-signal**: The packet creates a curvature difference larger than the prescribed signal threshold throughout the cosine-good part of the observation box. The result compares the two metrics and assumes no independent bound on background parallel-frame curvature.
- **invariant-averaged-holonomy-obstruction**: For parameters with a witness in the selected extension-chart collection, position and momentum averaging bounds the curvature block below the packet scale outside an exceptional set whose measure tends to zero. Chart control is established by stopping and improvement before the loop transport estimate is concluded.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-degenerate-kerr-limits**: Apply the generic inextendibility conclusion to nonrotating or extremal backgrounds, or assume its constants remain uniform toward those limits. -> The background neighborhood and comparison constants depend on a fixed rotating subextremal center. The paper explicitly excludes these limiting regimes.
- **anti-use-continuity-alone**: Treat the result as excluding every future extension with a merely continuous metric. -> The obstruction uses the locally square-integrable weak connection available from the specified Sobolev regularity. Continuity alone does not supply the chart connection bound used in the averaging argument.
- **anti-use-individual-sobolev-traces**: Evaluate the ambient weak connection on an arbitrarily prescribed curve and use that evaluation as a transport certificate. -> Almost-everywhere agreement of weak and smooth connection arrays does not define a trace on every prescribed curve. The proof removes exceptional parameters by position averaging and Fubini before applying smooth interior transport.
- **anti-use-template-as-vacuum-solution**: Substitute the double-null comparison template for the exact vacuum experiment or attach it using approximate entry matching. -> The template generally has controlled equation defects. Exact vacuum evolution begins with the actual prepared entry data, and the attachment identifies actual solutions through local geometric uniqueness.
- **anti-use-uniform-infinite-order-runtime**: Read the finite-order comparison as a uniform algorithm that computes all smooth derivatives with a single parameter choice or supplies a certified numerical evolution. -> The construction chooses finite derivative and accuracy budgets before taking the late-time limit. Smoothness at all remaining orders does not supply simultaneous subexponential estimates at all orders or a discrete error certificate.
- **anti-use-category-as-universal-or-open**: Infer that every nearby datum is future-inextendible, or that inextendibility is an open property from the residual conclusion. -> The theorem establishes meagreness of extendible data and a residual subset of inextendible data. It does not eliminate every exceptional datum or establish openness.
- **anti-use-position-only-overlap**: Estimate the overlap of the two good observing families using position volume alone or assume the seed-to-position map is injective. -> The comparison preserves canonical position–momentum section volume. Projecting away momentum can destroy the measure statement needed for overlap.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The paper proves an analytical category theorem for smooth vacuum developments and a conditional obstruction involving continuum families of positions, momenta, and smooth loop transports. A finite observation slab is still a continuum evolution problem; no finite computational representation or executable certification procedure for its hypotheses is supplied. The finite polynomial interpolation used inside the proof does not decide existence of the development, the extension witnesses, or the asserted generic inextendibility. The source states: "Every persistence assertion concerns a fixed finite observation; the argument does not require continuity of maximal geodesic lifetime." Thus a finite test harness would certify only an auxiliary computation, not these analytical invariants.
